- `wouldAnswer(from:to:at:)`: true when the step right above is a message the other way between the same two names and not an answer itself. Used for pulled and "Message to..." messages.
- `addMessageAfter(id)` (Return): right under it, from / to swapped, answer = the chosen one was not an answer (never for a message to itself).
- `moveStep(id, to:)`, `moveWho(id, to:)`: place counted without the moved thing; true when the place changed.
- `turnMessageRound(id)`: swaps from and to.
- `removeFromSequence(ids)`: a name takes every message from or to it; returns how many messages went along that were not chosen themselves. `messageCount(of:)`.
- `duplicateInSequence(ids)`: a step's copy right under it; a name's copy right beside it (no messages).
- `sequenceCountLine`: "4 people · 9 messages · 1 note · 1 section" ("Empty" for none; singular "1 person", "1 message").
- `stepLine(id)`: "2. Bank answers Alex: Yes"; `sequenceText` (Copy the board as text): "Who: Alex, Bank, Builder", then the numbered lines, "   Note: ...", "<section>:".
- Search on the home page finds names and words (they are ordinary item texts).

**Layout (`SequenceMetrics`, `SequenceLayout`; board points, the diagram's box starts at 0,0)**
- Margin left and right 28; above the names 20. Name box: name + 14 each side, at least 92 wide; height 34 or the tallest name + 16; corner 9.
- Dashed lines at least 150 apart, and at least half of both boxes + 24. Wider where needed: a message between neighbours needs number 18 + 5 + words + 14 each side; a message across several gaps shares what is missing evenly between those gaps; a message to itself needs 30 (loop) + 8 + number + words + 14 to the right (on the last name the diagram grows to the right).
- First step 12 under the names. A message: 9 above its words, words (17 a line, wrapping at 240), 4, the arrow, 9 under it: 39 for one line of words. A message to itself: loop 30 wide, 20 tall. A note: a band with 6 above and under its words, 6 room above and under the band; as wide as the diagram inside the margins; words wrap at 360. A section: 14 above, 8 under; its line runs across the whole width; words wrap at 420.
- The dashed lines end 40 under the last step, never shorter than 96; 16 under them.
- `slot(atY:moving:)`: the new place = how many other steps have their middle above the point. `landingY(slot:moving:)`: the edge between the two steps. `whoSlot(atX:moving:)`, `whoLandingX`. `who(nearX:reach:)`. `pinShift(top:inset:)`: how far the names move down to stay in sight.
- `Board.bounds` of a sequence = that box (nil when empty), so "whole board", the first view, the small picture and the PARAGON picture all use it.

**Look (`SequenceDraw.swift`, one painter for the open board, the home page picture and the PARAGON picture; `BoardPainter.draw` hands a sequence to `SequencePainter.whole`)**
- Name: 15 semibold, soft fill of its colour, outline 1.7 in its colour, a faint shadow by day. Dashed line: 1.3, dashes 5/5, the name's colour at 65 %.
- Message: line 1.8 in its own colour or the colour of the name it comes from; filled head 9.5 x 9.2; a 5-point dot where it starts. Answer: dashes 6/4, open head. Number: a round mark 18 across, bold 10.5, white by day and dark by night. Words 14, on a patch of the board's own colour so a dashed line never runs through the letters. To do: PARAGON's purple ring with a tick, 11 right of the words.
- Note: soft fill (yellow when it has no colour), outline at 50 %, words 13.5 centred. Section: line 1.2, words 13.5 semibold.
- Chosen: a green ring 2; a message also gets a soft green glow along its line. "Message to..." start and the name a pull heads for: a dashed green ring. Dragged thing: drawn at 80 % where the finger is, its place at 30 %.
- Pinned names: 8 points under the top edge (52 while a line of words lies over the top), on a strip of the board's colour with a hairline under it.
- New app part `Part.sequence`: olive green #5C7A12 by day, #B5D95A by night (Who tool, Answer, Colour, Move up / down).

**Screen (`SequenceView.swift`, `SequenceSession.swift`, `SequenceBars.swift`)**
- `BoardView` shows `SequenceView` when `board.isSequence` (`isTimeline` became `placesItself` in three places); tools `SequenceToolPill`: `tool.who`, `tool.message`, `tool.note`, `tool.section` (4 tools with their word under them: Who, Message, Note, Section); bar `SequenceSelectionBar`; zoom buttons, Undo, top bar, Send as on every board. Empty board: `sequence.empty` "Nobody here yet" with one line of help.
- Bars: for a message `sel.words`, `sel.answer`, `sel.turn`, `sel.colour`, `sel.todo`, `sel.copy` ("Duplicate", which puts a copy under it), `sel.remove`, and on iPhone and iPad `sel.moveUp`, `sel.moveDown`; for a name `sel.name`, `sel.colour`, `sel.messageTo`, `sel.remove`; `sel.more` on iPhone and iPad.
- What a drag does: sideways from a dashed line (within 11 screen points of it) = pull a message; sideways on a name box = move it in the row; up or down on a step = move it in the order (iPhone / iPad: after resting 0.25 s, with a light tap of the motor); anything else slides the view. A pull lands on the nearest dashed line within 44 screen points (at most 0.4 of 150); back on its own line only after having been 30 screen points away = a message to itself; let go on nothing = nothing. While the pull runs, the target name has a dashed ring and a thin line shows the slot.
- Press: chooses; twice = type; Shift-press (Mac) or Choose more (iPhone / iPad) adds to what is chosen. A press on a dashed line chooses its name. While a message is being made only names (or their lines) count; a press on empty board cancels.
- Keys (nothing being typed): W, M, N, S tools; Return = next message (on a message) or type (others); Tab on a name = next name; the arrow keys choose (up, down: step; left, right: name); Option + arrows move; Space = type; Delete; Escape; ⌘A; any letter starts typing in the chosen thing. While typing: Return = done, Escape = cancel, Tab = next name / next message. iPhone / iPad keyboard bar: `board.key.nextWho` "Next name" or `board.key.nextMessage` "Next message", and Done (`board.fieldDone`).
- Every change is one Undo step (`change`); typing as on every board. Removing a name shows the line "“Bank” went with its 2 messages." with Undo.
- `BoardSession` hooks (one line each): `paste` (does nothing on a sequence), `deleteSelection`, `duplicateSelection`, `toggleTodo` (messages only), `reveal`. Other shared files touched lightly: `Board.swift`, `BoardEdit.swift`, `Outline.swift`, `Paragon.swift`, `ProjectPlan.swift`, `Starters.swift`, `Theme.swift`, `CanvasDraw.swift`, `SendSheet.swift`, `BoardThumb.swift` (the timeline starter's date), `Sample.swift`, `SafetyTests.swift`, `StartersTests.swift`.
- Identifiers: `sequence.canvas`, `sequence.empty`, `sequence.chosen` (the chosen thing in words, invisible), `board.count`, `board.field`, `board.tools`, `board.selectionBar`, `board.colours`, `colour.none`, `colour.<name>`, and those named above. All existing identifiers are kept.

**Send (Sequence.swift, `ParagonExport`; 5)**
- `messageLine`: "Alex → Bank: Ask for loan"; an answer "Bank answers Alex: Yes"; to itself "Bank, by itself: Check the papers"; no words: ends after the names; a name without words is "(no name)".
- Note (`sequenceNote`, Resources): front matter, `# title`, the picture line, the link back, `## Who` (names joined with " · "), `## Who does what` (a numbered list; a note as `> ...`, a section as `### ...`, blank lines round both), `## Tasks` (ticked messages as `- [ ] Alex → Builder: Order the build`).
- Project (`sequenceProject`, Projects): `## Outcome` (the title), `## Tasks` (every message in order: `- [ ] ...` for a task, `- ...` for a plain line; sections as `###`), `## Notes` ("Who: ...", the notes as bullets), `## Log`.
- Ticks: with at least one ticked message only ticked ones are tasks; with none all are (`sequenceTicksDecide`, `sequenceTaskLines`). Send page line (`sequenceProjectLine`): "2 ticked messages become tasks, 7 stay as plain lines." / "Nothing is ticked: all 9 messages become tasks."
- Only the to-do boxes: the generic rule (ticked items with words, not yet sent); only messages can be ticked. They go with their bare words ("Order the build"), without the two names.
- The picture: `BoardPainter.draw` hands a sequence to `SequencePainter.whole`.

**Fixed on the way**: the Timeline starter's small picture on the home page was blank, because its example was made for 1 January 2001 and the picture always includes today, so 25 years had to fit into 240 points. The example is now made for today (`BoardThumb.swift`).

**Not built / for another builder**: the Mac's Board menu items "Line Up", "Even Gaps", "Same Size" and "Paste a List as a Mind Map" are not switched off on a sequence board (nor on a timeline); paste does nothing and the three tidy commands would only write places nothing reads (one empty Undo step); they should be disabled where `board.placesItself`. A note always runs across all the lines (a note over one or two names is not built). Words in a message's typing field are one line while typing, also when they wrap to two lines once done. No Mac menu lines "Move the Message Up / Down" (Option with the arrow keys instead, because ⌘↑ / ⌘↓ belong to the idea commands). The Note and Section tools show stand-in marks (`.new(.lanes)`, the sticky-note mark) until drawn marks arrive.

**Tests**: `SequenceTests.swift`, 17 data tests (file round trip, starter, numbering, inserting, Return, pulled answer, reorder by y, reorder the row, turn round, remove a name, copy, spacing, message to itself, note and section, nearest line and pinning, note hand-over, project hand-over). `UITests/SequenceUITests.swift`: 1 screen test, written, not run.

### 3.17 Pictures (Pictures.swift, PictureStore.swift, PictureViews.swift, ClipBoard.swift; the screens: 2.10)

- Data: `ItemKind.picture` (stored name "picture", word "Picture") and the item field `picture`: the name of the file (`<UUID>.jpg`). x, y, w, h are the picture's frame on the board as for any thing. Format 3 (3.2). In 0.3 a picture had no words; since 0.4 its `text` is its caption (3.20). A picture has no colour, no To do, no `todo`, and is not a shape (`isShape` false).
- `Board.pictureSize(forPixels:)`: a new picture keeps its shape; its long side is `min(320, max(longest side in pixels, 120))` board points (`pictureLongest` 320, `pictureShortest` 120), sizes rounded; a picture of 0 pixels falls back to 240 x 180. `Board.addPicture(name:pixelSize:centre:)` lays it with its middle at the point; it is refused (nil) on a timeline and on a Who does what board (`placesItself`), on a board that cannot be changed, and for a name that is not a plain file name (`PictureKeeping.isPlainName`: 1 to 100 characters, ASCII letters, digits, dot, dash, underscore; no leading dot, no `..`). The picture is chosen at once.
- Resize (`Board.scalePicture(_:to:)`, called from `setFrame` in `BoardEdit.swift`): a corner square is dragged; the shape is kept (the larger of the two scales wins), the corner opposite the dragged one stays, and the picture is never smaller than 44 x 28 points.
- Arrows can start and end at a picture; the arrow router goes round it like round a shape (`Arrows.swift`). Line up and Even gaps include pictures; Same size leaves them out (their shape is fixed). Copy, Cut, Paste (Seedboard's own form), Duplicate and Remove act as for any thing; Remove leaves the file (below). Several pictures pasted or dropped at one spot land 28 points further right and down each.
- Count line: "1 picture" / "2 pictures" after the other parts (`Board.pictureCount`). "Copy the board as text" and the outline write a picture as "A picture" (a list line under Notes: "- A picture"). For PARAGON: the board's picture (`BoardPicture`, Share, Export and the small pictures of the list) draws the pictures; the note's and the project's text give one plain line under Notes, "A picture (see the picture)" (or "A picture (on the board)" — see 9.5); a picture is never a task and has no To do. The 0.2 sketch line is unchanged.
- Ways in. (1) The tool, the key P, Insert ▸ Picture… and the empty-board menu line (2.10). (2) Paste: ⌘V, Edit ▸ Paste, the board's menu. A screenshot or copied picture is laid in the middle of the view (or at the pressed spot); on the Mac image files copied in the Finder count too. If the clipboard holds words and no copied file, the WORDS win (a copied table or web page carries a picture of itself too). (3) Drop (Mac and iPad, the same `.onDrop` as dropped words): an image file or an image from another app lands where it is dropped. A picture that cannot be read as an image is counted and said (2.10).
- The file (`PictureStore`). A picture that comes in is scaled so its long side is at most 2048 pixels, transparency is flattened onto white, and it is saved as a JPEG of quality 0.8 under a fresh name `<UUID>.jpg` (`PictureKeeping.freshName`). A file is written ONCE and never changed. Place: a folder `Pictures` beside `Boards`: on the device `Application Support/Pictures`, and in iCloud `Documents/Pictures` (found through the same container as the boards). Sample mode (`-sampleData`) uses the temporary folder `SeedboardSamplePictures` and never touches iCloud. A file still only in iCloud is asked for (`startDownloadingUbiquitousItem`); the board redraws when it arrives. The picture cache is capped at 160 MB.
- Removing a picture from a board never deletes its file (Undo must work, a copy may name it, another device may still have the board).
- Tidying (`PictureKeeping.deletable`, `PictureStore.tidy`): once per start of the app, 25 seconds after launch, a file in the Pictures folder (device and iCloud) goes only when BOTH hold: it is older than 30 days (`keepDays`, by its modification date) and no board names it — the open boards, the removed boards (`Removed/`), the safety copies and earlier versions (`SafetyCopies/`) are searched as plain bytes for the file name. In any doubt nothing is deleted: a copy that cannot be read, a placeholder still only in iCloud, a file with no date. **The starters are searched too: `Starters/` beside each Pictures folder is one of the roots read (a starter's board may name a picture).** A starter made from a board with pictures names the same files, and a starter that names a file keeps it.
- Not in this version: pictures on a timeline or Who does what board, a picture in the Export file (see 9.5), pictures in the "Copy as text" other than the one word.
- Sample: `-sampleData -board balcony` opens the made-up board "Balcony makeover" (teal; two landscapes drawn in code at 1200 x 800 pixels, two notes "Herbs along the railing" and "Order planters and a small table", one arrow "becomes"); `-pickPicture` chooses the first picture (handled in `PictureHost`).
- Tests: `PictureTests`, 8 data tests (size rules; adding and refusal; corner resize; copy and duplicate; count line and outline; plain names; the keeping rule; the file format number); screen test `PictureUITests` 1 (a chosen picture's bar has no words and no colour; via `-pickPicture`).

### 3.18 Starters of your own (StarterShelf.swift, StarterStore.swift, StarterModel.swift; the screens: 2.10)

- `StarterEntry` (one stored starter): `id` (a built-in's raw value such as "mindMap", or a UUID string for an own starter), `name`, `hint` (the grey line), `order` (Double), `removed`, `removedWhen`, `builtin` (the built-in it belongs to; nil for an own starter), `board` (nil for a built-in never changed), `changed` (date). Reading is tolerant (missing name "Starter", missing `changed` 1970); fields it does not know are carried and written back.
- `StarterShelf`: a dictionary of entries. `everything` is every starter by order, `shown` those not removed. A built-in with no entry is shown as it always was (the seven in their old order, with their old name and hint). `ShelfStarter` is what screens see: `isBuiltin`, `isChangedBuiltin` (a built-in with its own board), `canBeRemoved` (false only for the Blank board: a new board must always be possible), `missingBuiltins` (a built-in is removed: "Bring back the built-in starters" is offered).
- Rules: `rename(id, name:hint:)`; `move(id, to:)` (an index in the shown list as it stands without it); `remove(id)` (false for Blank; sets `removed` and `removedWhen`); `bringBackBuiltins()` (every removed built-in is shown again, with whatever board, name and place it had); `backToOriginal(id)` (a changed built-in loses its board; name, hint and place stay); `change(id, board:)` (stores a changed board, for a built-in or an own starter); `saveAs(board, name:hint:)` makes an own starter that goes last and returns its id; `freeName(wanted, ignoring:)` → "Name", else "Name 2", "Name 3" … among shown starters; `purge` / `purgeable`: own starters removed more than 30 days ago (`keepRemoved`).
- A new board from a starter (`makeBoard(from:)`, `StarterShelf.fresh`): a copy with new ids everywhere (items, arrows and all references re-pointed), titled like the starter, `created` and `changed` now, `pinned` false, `sent` nil, `sentTodos` empty. A timeline board moves by whole days (today minus the day it was last changed), so the plan sits as far from today as when the starter was saved. A built-in never changed is made by `Starter.board()` as before. Names: `AppModel.add(_:)` still numbers a taken title ("Trip plan 2").
- The working board for changing (`workingBoard(for:)`): the starter's board with the same ids, and a timeline moved to sit round today, so that saving it again (which dates it today) keeps the plan as far from today as it looks.
- Files (`StarterStore`): one small JSON file per starter that was ever changed or made, `Starters/<id>.seedstarter`, beside `Boards`: on the device (Application Support) and in iCloud (the same container, Documents). A removal keeps its file (`removed: true`) so it reaches every device; a removed own starter's file is deleted 30 days after the removal. For the same id the newer `changed` wins. A file still arriving from iCloud (`.name.seedstarter.icloud`) is asked for. The shelf is read at the start, when the app comes to the front and when the Starters page opens. Sample mode and the unit tests keep the shelf in memory only; sample mode has two own starters, "Weekly review" (id `00000000-0000-0000-0000-0000000000A1`) and "Trip plan" (`…A2`).
- Saving a board as a starter takes the board as it is (the open board from the live session; `boardForStarter`), not one that cannot be changed. A board that holds pictures gives a starter that names the same picture files (3.17).
- Not touched: Shortcuts and Siri (`StarterChoice` is the seven built-in starters, as in 0.2); a Spotlight entry; the old Starters.swift.
- Launch words (with `-sampleData`): `-starters` (the starter choice, 0.2), `-starterPage` (the Starters page), `-starterChange <id or name>` (opens that starter for changing, e.g. `-starterChange mindMap` or `-starterChange "Trip plan"`).
- Tests: `StarterShelfTests`, 15 data tests (the shelf as it was, rename, move, remove and the Blank board, bring back, back to the original, change, save as, free names, a fresh board, a timeline moving by days, newer wins, purge after 30 days, tolerant reading); screen test `StartersUITests` 1 (the page lists every starter and closes with Done; via `-starterPage`).

### 3.19 Small fixes of 0.3 (FixesTests.swift; ProjectPlan.swift, Sequence.swift, TimelineSession.swift, BoardSession.swift, TrackpadCatcher.swift)

1. A Who does what message with a missing name. 0.2: a message naming someone who is not on the board (a file made elsewhere) was counted (the list line, the numbers, the copied text, the PARAGON note and project) but never drawn, and Select all and Up/Down could land on it. 0.3: a message counts only when BOTH its names are on the board. It is left out of the count line, the numbers (1, 2, 3 follow the drawn ones), the copied text, what is sent and the "ticked messages decide" rule; Select all (`selectAll`) and Up / Down (`selectStep`) skip it. It stays in the file untouched. Test `FixesTests`.
2. Mac: Actual Size ⌘0 on a timeline. 0.2: nothing happened (it asked for a zoom of 1 on a board whose zoom is days, weeks, months). 0.3: it goes back to the standard size, the Weeks scale it opens in (`TimelineSession.actualSize`), the day in the middle staying put. ⌘1 (the whole plan) is as before. On a Who does what board ⌘0 is life size and ⌘1 the whole diagram, as before. No line is greyed out.
3. A small screen's opening spot on a Who does what board (`Board.mainThing`). The spot a board opens on at 75 % on an iPhone took the first item's own stored place, which means nothing on that board (every item sits at 0, 0), so the opening was centred on the wrong place. 0.3: it opens on the first name's box as the layout puts it. Sure of the cause; the change is a few points and was not seen on a screen.
4. Send to PARAGON as a project, the tally (`ProjectPlan`). Shapes inside the lanes of a frame were counted as ideas ("all 2 ideas become tasks"); now they count as shapes ("Nothing is ticked: all 2 shapes become tasks."), as the flow shapes outside lanes do. Test `FixesTests`.
5. Narrow iPhones, tool rows of the timeline and the Who does what board: worked out and found to fit (375 wide: about 264 and about 236 of 355 usable points; a 320-wide SE also). Nothing changed.
6. Mac: key S for Draw (3.15, 2.6).
7. iPad with a trackpad or a mouse on Timeline and Who does what boards: `TrackpadCatcher` (iPad only; ordinary boards already had it in 0.2 and `ScrollCatcher` is Mac only): a scroll recogniser with no touch types and all scroll types, on the screen's top view, answering only inside the board's box: two-finger scroll or the wheel slides the board, ⌘ + scroll zooms round the pointer. A trackpad pinch relies on the boards' existing `MagnifyGesture` (no second pinch recogniser, to avoid zooming twice). Not provable in the simulator (9.5).

### 3.20 Caption and crop (Pictures.swift; PictureViews.swift, CropPage.swift; the screens: 2.11)

- Stored field `crop` (format 4, 3.2): `Item.crop: [Double]?`, four numbers 0 to 1 (x, y, width, height of the shown part, as a part of the whole picture). Cleaned when read and when set (`Item.cleanCrop`): not four finite numbers, or a part smaller than 0.05 wide or high, gives nil; a part reaching outside the picture is cut back to it; the whole picture (0, 0, 1, 1) is nil (so it is left out of the file). `Item.shownPart` is the cleaned crop or the whole picture.
- `Board.cropPicture(_ id:, to part: [Double]?) -> Bool`: shows only `part`, or everything for nil. The scale stays (a point of the picture covers as many board points as before) and the kept part stays exactly where it was on the board: the whole picture's size on the board is `w / old width part`, `h / old height part`; the box's x, y, w, h are then worked out from the new part. Refused (false) for a thing that is not a picture, a board that cannot be changed, a picture of no size, and a part that is not four finite numbers, is smaller than 0.05 wide or high, or lies outside the picture (0.0001 allowed). Whole picture, then Save, brings everything back; the file is never touched.
- Resizing a cropped picture by a corner keeps the cropped shape. Duplicate, Copy and Paste carry the crop and the caption.
- Caption: the picture's own `text` (no new field). `Item.captionWords` is the trimmed text of a picture ("" for any other kind). It stands `Item.captionGap` 6 points under the picture, in the middle, as wide as the picture, 12 points, grey like other quiet text; longer words wrap. It is not part of the picture's box: the chosen frame and corner knobs stay on the picture; the caption moves, zooms and is copied with it. `Item.captionFrame` = the room kept (6 below the picture, as wide as the picture, `captionLines` x `captionLine` (15) + 2 high); `Item.reach` = the picture's frame joined with it. `Item.captionLines(words, width:)` is a careful guess of the lines (6.6 points a letter, at least 4 letters a line, every line break counts). A press on the caption chooses the picture (`BoardEdit.swift`).
- What gives the caption room (`reach`): `Board.bounds` (Show the whole board), the picture for PARAGON, Share and Export, the small pictures of the list, and the mind map's growth (`Room.swift`), which moves a note, shape or picture aside when it would cover a picture's caption. Line up, Even gaps and every other move work on the picture's box only.
- Search (`Home.swift`) finds a caption. "Copy the board as text" and the outline write "A picture: After the makeover" (and "A picture" without a caption, as before). The PARAGON note or project text: "A picture: After the makeover (see the picture)" or "(on the board)" (`Paragon.swift`); without a caption as in 3.17. A picture still has no To do.
- Sample: the second picture of "Balcony makeover" is captioned "After: herbs and a small table".
- The example person in the "Who does what" starter (`Sequence.swift`) and in the sample boards (`Sample.swift`, `SequenceSample.swift`) is "Alex" (a neutral name since 0.4). Nothing else about the starter changed.
- The amber dot waits for pictures (0.4; `PictureStore.swift` "Not in iCloud yet", `AppModel.waitsForCloud`, `RootView.swift`, `SettingsView.swift`; rule 11 of section 6 extends). 0.3: the dot at the end of a board's row and the line in Settings, Your boards, meant only that the BOARD's newest version was not in iCloud yet. 0.4: the dot stays until every picture the board shows is in iCloud too. While pictures wait, Settings, Your boards, has a second line (`settings.picturesNotInCloud`): "1 picture is not in iCloud yet. It is safe on this device." / "N pictures are not in iCloud yet. They are safe on this device." Checked every 3 seconds while something waits. At the start of the app a picture that never reached iCloud's Pictures folder (a failed write) is copied there again. Not seen with real iCloud.
- The starter choice (the pop-up or page New board opens): heading "Starters" (large, centred, `starters.heading`), Edit small at the right (`starters.edit`). 0.3: "Start with", small, at the left. The Shortcuts action's field stays "Start with".
- Icons: two new hand-drawn marks, Crop and Caption (`NewMarks.swift`: `.crop`, `.caption`; the library has 336 drawings, pinned revision `12961b604e22ab95b60217c6849384ff1eb28518`; `.seedText` and `.seedFrame` are stand-ins until the lead swaps them).
- Limits: (1) the room kept for a caption is a guess; on a very narrow picture with long words the screen may take one line more, and the last line can reach a little past the box that Show the whole board or the PARAGON picture measures. (2) Only mind-map growth makes room for captions; Line up, Even gaps and dragging work on the picture's box. (3) A board with a crop is format 4: Seedboard 0.3 and older show it but cannot change it.
- Identifiers: `sel.caption`, `sel.crop`, `menu.caption`, `menu.crop`, `crop.page`, `crop.frame`, `crop.grip.<corner>` (topLeft, topRight, bottomLeft, bottomRight), `crop.cancel`, `crop.whole`, `crop.save`. Launch word `-cropPicture`.
- Tests: `CaptionCropTests`, 7 data tests (crop keeps scale and place, whole picture again, cleaning and refusal, caption words and room, search and text, PARAGON line, format 4); `StoredFieldsTests` has the format-4 list; screen test `PictureUITests.testCropOpensItsPageAndCancelClosesIt` (crop page opens via `-cropPicture` and Cancel closes it).

## 4. Look

- Parts and colours (day / night; 0.3 adds pictures 0A7FA6 / 62C6EC): boards green 2E8B57 / 5FBF8A; mind map A8388F / E27FC8; shapes 2B67C9 / 7FAEF5; arrows 4A5565 / A9B4C4; notes B77A08 / F0BE50; frames 178378 / 5CCBBE; PARAGON 6B4EA8 / B59CEB; timeline C4551F / F59A66; removed and earlier versions (`Part.kept`) B4492B / F0A183; text = ink. A full-colour button always uses the day shade with white words; disabled = half opacity, not grey. Soft backgrounds are 6 % (day) or 12 % (night) of the colour over a pale base.
- Calmer colours of 0.2: the Send button is PARAGON purple (2.3) — everything that leads to PARAGON is purple; the chosen row is a soft tint (2.2) and "New board" is the only solid green in the list; Flow and Plan in steps come coloured (3.3); shapes have a faint shadow by day (3.5); sticky notes keep bright paper and dark ink at night (3.1); a ring marks the colour in use (2.3).
- Guides pink E0457B / F58FB2; dots D5DAE2 / 2C2F36 (3.11).
- Type: Apple text styles; on the Mac titles take a step up. Board text 17 / 15 / 13 as in 3.4 and 3.5.
- Marks: hand-drawn marks from the AMS-Icons package (app marks such as seedSprout, seedBox, seedMindMap, seedArrow, seedNote, seedText, seedFrame, seedFit, send, undo, redo, duplicate, bin, palette, zoomIn, zoomOut; control marks add, chevron, ticked, circle, edit, settings, close). New in 0.3: the marks Picture (`Glyph.new(.picture)`, the tool, the plate and the menu line) and Save as a starter (the Starters page uses `.app(.seedStarter)`, and `.new(.bringBack)` for Bring back), hand-drawn in the AMS-Icons package; the icon library now holds 334 drawings, the package is pinned to revision d80e15dc8a896cf3358e20604eea1cca8867698b. The part colour of pictures is `Part.pictures` (day #0A7FA6, night #62C6EC). New marks of 0.2 are named in `NewMarks.swift` (`Glyph.new(…)`: lineUp, evenGaps, sameSize, search, bar, milestone, today, calendar, timeline, moveUp, moveDown, ideaUnder, nextIdea, outLevel, branch, oneWay, bothWays, noEnds, dashed, person, document, slanted, store, lanes, bringBack, earlier, export). Two marks are still wanted (the timeline builder's `ICON-WANTED`): a phase (now the frame mark) and "comes after" (now the arrow mark). The Shortcuts list of the system requires a system symbol name for each phrase group (`text.badge.plus`, `plus.rectangle`, `rectangle.stack`); they show only in the Shortcuts app and the device's search, never inside Seedboard. The app icon: a white hand-drawn seed with three branches on a flat teal tile, no transparency.
- A to-do box shows its mark at its upper right corner (a branch idea: beside the idea; a Person: beside the head; a timeline row: at the right of its name). **0.5:** four marks by state, 11.14 (0.4: always the full purple ring with a white tick).
- Settings headings: Help green, PARAGON purple, Your boards green, About teal.
- Day and night follow the system (`-night` forces night for pictures).

## 5. Send to PARAGON (Paragon.swift, ParagonGate.swift, SendSheet.swift)

**0.6:** a done bar or milestone is sent ticked (`- [x]`, with `@done(day)`), never to the Inbox, and with no Start: line (12.1).

**0.5:** Seedboard now also READS PARAGON's notes (11.14), aims waiting lines at an existing note for "Add what is new" (PARAGON writes them itself, 11.15), and the texts gain Doors, frame headings, "(already in the Inbox)", the way back and "Start:" tasks (11.16). Both rules below still hold: Seedboard never opens an existing PARAGON file for writing.

Two rules protect the owner's notes: Seedboard only ever adds new files to PARAGON's folder (it never opens, changes or replaces an existing one; files are written without overwriting; the one file it may take away again is a picture it made itself a moment ago in the same hand-over, when the note for it could not be written), and Inbox lines are never written into `Inbox.md` but into the waiting list that PARAGON's own Share button uses.

### 5.1 Finding PARAGON

- PARAGON's notes folder: the `Documents` folder of the iCloud container `iCloud.com.schabbauer.AMSPara`. It counts as PARAGON's folder IN USE when it holds `Inbox.md` (or `.Inbox.md.icloud`) or PARAGON's own settings file `.ams-para/config.json` (or `.ams-para/.config.json.icloud`) — `ParagonExport.looksLikeParagonFolder`. A folder with only a `Projects` folder in it is not taken: that can be a folder PARAGON never used. (Seedboard cannot ask PARAGON which folder it uses when the owner pointed PARAGON at a folder of his own; only this container is looked at.)
- The Inbox waiting list: the file `capture-outbox.jsonl` in the app-group container `group.com.schabbauer.amspara` (macOS: `D24ENP83QQ.group.com.schabbauer.amspara`). If the group container cannot be reached, notes and projects can still be sent and the Inbox cannot.
- Without iCloud (no container) PARAGON is "not found". With `-sampleData` PARAGON is "not found" (unless `-paragonFolder`, below).
- Looking is slow the first time and runs off the main thread; it runs at launch, on Settings → Check again, and again at EVERY Send (the folder may have appeared, gone or changed since the app started); the PARAGON line in Settings follows what the last look found.
- Entitlements: sandbox, user-selected files read-write, iCloud documents with both containers (Seedboard's own first, PARAGON's second), and the app group above (iPhone/iPad: `project.yml`; Mac: `App/Config/AMSSeedboard-macOS.entitlements`). `LSApplicationQueriesSchemes`: `amspara`.

### 5.2 Names

- Note title (`noteTitle`): `/ \ : * ? " < > |`, line breaks, tabs and control characters become a space; spaces squeezed; dots and spaces trimmed at both ends; at most 100 characters (re-trimmed); `(conflict` becomes `(clash` (PARAGON reads "(conflict …)" as a conflict copy); an empty title becomes "Board"; the words "capture" and "timeblock" (any case) become "<word> (board)".
- Free title (`freeTitle`): compared with every `.md` file name in the PARAGON folder (all sub-folders, hidden folders skipped, `.icloud` placeholders counted), case-insensitive. If taken: "Title (board 2)", "(board 3)"… A name that would look like "Name 2" (a single digit 2–9 after a space) beside an existing "Name" is also treated as taken.
- Picture name: `Seedboard <safe title, at most 60 characters> <yyyy-MM-dd HHmm>.png` (letters, digits, space, `- _ . ,` kept; others become spaces); empty → "board". If it exists: " b2", " b3"… (up to 50 tries).

### 5.3 What is written where (all relative to PARAGON's folder)

- As a note: `Resources/<Title>.md`, and `Pictures/<picture>.png`.
- As a project: `Projects/<Title>.md`, and `Pictures/<picture>.png`.
- The picture is written first; if it fails nothing else is written and the page says "The picture could not be saved in PARAGON's folder. Nothing was changed." If the note then fails, the picture made a moment ago (only that new file) is taken away again and the page says "The note could not be saved in PARAGON's folder. Nothing was changed."; if the picture cannot be taken away either: "The note could not be saved in PARAGON's folder. Its picture “<name>” is left in PARAGON's Pictures folder; no note uses it."  The picture is made from the board in the day look on a white background (the plates behind words on arrows are white too), the bounds grown by 40, at 2x (1x when the larger side is over 3000 points). Nothing is picture-written for "Only the to-do boxes". An empty board has no picture (and the Send page does not allow it).
- Only the to-do boxes: no file in the folder; lines go to the waiting list.
- The waiting list is JSON Lines: one line per entry, appended in one step (`O_APPEND`), so PARAGON and Seedboard cannot write into each other. Each line: `id` (new UUID), `text`, `target` (inbox), `asTask` (true for a task, false for a note line), `createdAt` (ISO 8601; task lines are 1 s apart in board order). The lines are made in SeedboardCore (`ParagonExport.WaitingLine`, `taskLines`, `noteLine`, `waitingData`), and a test reads them back with a copy of PARAGON's own reader (`CaptureItem`, PARAGON build 339). All the lines of one send are written in ONE append: either every task is on its way, or none (a failed write says the Inbox cannot be reached). PARAGON files the lines itself the next time it opens.
- As a note: after the note is written, one line `[[<Title>]] — a board from Seedboard` with `asTask` false is added, only when the waiting list is reachable (a failure to write it is ignored). The result message: "“<Title>” is in PARAGON under Resources, with a line in the Inbox." or "… under Resources." or "… under Projects."
- Only the to-do boxes: one task per unsent to-do item that has words, in board order. So that the same tasks can never go twice, the "already sent" ticks are saved FIRST: the board with the ticks is written to the device (and waited for) before the lines are added to the waiting list; if adding them fails, the board is stored again without the ticks. A second Send while one is on its way is refused ("The board is on its way already."). Message: "1 task is on the way to the PARAGON Inbox. They show the next time PARAGON opens." (or "N tasks are…"). Errors: "No to-do boxes are waiting to be sent." / "This device cannot reach PARAGON's Inbox. Send the board as a note or a project instead."
- Afterwards (`markSent`): for a note or project the board's `sent` is set (when, kind, title, path "<Folder>/<Title>.md") and EVERY to-do item (also ones whose words are empty) is added to `sentTodos`; for to-do boxes only the sent ones are added. The board is saved at once. Taking the To do off a box (`toggleTodo`, all chosen ticked → cleared) removes it from `sentTodos`, so ticking it again sends it again. Copies of a board start with `sentTodos` empty.
- Open in PARAGON: opens `amspara://<title, percent-encoded>`.
- Board link: `seedboard://board/<board UUID>` (what happens when it arrives: 2.2). In PARAGON it opens from the reading view, and since PARAGON build 339 also while the note is edited (iPhone and iPad: a tap on its words; Mac: ⌘-click).

### 5.4 Text of a note (`Resources/`)

```
---
title: <Title>
type: resource
created: yyyy-MM-dd
---
# <Title>

![Picture](../Pictures/<picture, %-encoded>)

[Open the board in Seedboard](seedboard://board/<UUID>)

## Outline
- <centre>
  - <branch>
    - <idea>

## Flow
- <from> → <to> (<words on the arrow>)

## Notes
- <words of a sticky note or free text>

## Tasks
- [ ] <words of each item marked To do>
```

A value that starts with `[ { " '` in the front matter is quoted. A section with no lines is left out. The picture line is left out when there is no picture. Words of one item are made one line (line breaks become one space, runs of spaces one space); an empty label reads "(empty)". Outline: every mind map, each idea a bullet, two spaces per level. Flow: every arrow whose two ends exist, in arrow order. Notes: notes and free text with words, in item order. Tasks: every item marked To do that has words (any kind), in item order. The four new shapes are flow shapes: they appear in "Flow" lines exactly like a Box. A frame with lanes adds its own section after "Flow" (5.8). A timeline board has its own note text (5.9).

### 5.5 Text of a project (`Projects/`)

```
---
title: <Title>
type: project
status: active
created: yyyy-MM-dd
---
# <Title>

![Picture](../Pictures/…)

[Open the board in Seedboard](seedboard://board/<UUID>)

## Outcome

<words of the first map's centre that has words, else the title>

## Tasks

- [ ] <a main branch with no ideas under it>

### <main branch with ideas under it>
- [ ] <idea>
  - [ ] <deeper idea>

### Steps
- [ ] <each flow shape with words, in arrow order>

### To do
- [ ] <each note or free text marked To do>

## Notes

- <notes and free text>

## Log

- yyyy-MM-dd: Project created from a Seedboard board
```

All main branches of all maps are used. The text above is what is written when NO idea is marked To do: every idea under a main branch becomes a task (as in 0.1). When at least one idea is marked, the ticks decide (5.6). Steps: every shape with words (any of the eight kinds, marked or not): those no arrow points at first, each followed along its arrows, then any left over; shapes that lie in a lane are listed under their lane instead (5.8). To do: notes and free text marked To do (ideas and shapes are already in the lists above). Nothing goes to the Inbox for a project.

### 5.6 A project by the ticks (`ProjectPlan.swift`)

- `ParagonExport.ticksDecide(board)`: true when at least one *idea* has `todo`. False → the text is exactly as in 5.5.
- True:
  - a ticked centre adds `- [ ] <centre>` before everything else (it is still the Outcome);
  - an unticked main branch with ideas under it → `### <branch>`, then its ideas; with none → a plain `- <branch>` before the headings;
  - a ticked main branch → `- [ ] <branch>` before the headings, its ideas indented under it;
  - every idea below: ticked → `- [ ] <words>`, unticked → `- <words>`;
  - indent (two spaces per level): a plain line stands one level below the line of the idea it hangs under; a task stands one level below the nearest *ticked* idea above it in the branch, and at the left edge when there is none. Reason: PARAGON hangs an indented task under the nearest task above it with less indent and passes over plain lines (`TaskParser.parse(text:)`), so a ticked idea under an unticked one would otherwise become a sub-task of an unrelated task;
  - `### Steps`: every shape with words in arrow order, ticked → `- [ ]`, unticked → `- `;
  - `### To do`: ticked notes and free text, as before.
- Example: the sample board "Summer cabin" gave 13 tasks in 0.1; with its two ticks it now gives 2 tasks ("Permit", "Check timber prices in May") and 11 plain lines.
- `ParagonExport.projectTally(board:)` → `ProjectTally { ticks, ideaTasks, ideaPlain, shapeTasks, shapePlain, otherTasks, anyTick }`, counted from the very lines `project()` writes.
- `ParagonExport.projectLine(board:)` (the Send page's line `send.projectLine`, 2.4): when the ticks decide: "N ticked ideas become tasks" ("things" instead of "ideas" when a ticked shape, note or text is among them; "1 ticked idea becomes a task"), then ", M stay as plain lines" (", 1 stays as a plain line"; left out when 0), full stop. Otherwise: "Nothing is ticked: all N ideas become tasks." / "… all N ideas and M shapes become tasks." / "… the 1 idea becomes a task."; it starts "No idea is ticked:" when a note, text or shape is ticked but no idea; no line when the project would hold neither ideas nor shapes.

### 5.7 Send this branch (`ProjectPlan.swift`, `SendBranch.swift`)

- `Board.branchOnly(_ id:) -> Board?`: the same board `id` (the link back opens the whole board), `title` = the idea's words in one line (the board's title when it has none), `sent` = nil, `sentTodos` kept; items = the idea and everything below it; arrows only between those. An idea that was not a centre becomes one (`parent` nil, one side); the ideas right under it take the colour the branch had; then `layoutMaps()`. nil for anything that is not an idea. `Board.branchName(_:)` = the idea's words in one line.
- When the switch of 2.4 is on: the counts, the project line, the name and Send all use the branch. The name follows the switch (`noteTitle(for:)` of the branch's words / of the board's title) unless the owner typed another name.
- `AppModel.send(kind:title:branch:)`: sends `branchOnly(branch)`; the picture is drawn from it and named after the branch ("Seedboard Budget yyyy-MM-dd HHmm.png"). Afterwards `markSent(nil, todos:)`: the board's `sent` is NOT set (it does not move to Sent to PARAGON and shows no "Last sent" line); the ticks that went with the branch are added to `sentTodos`.
- `ParagonGate` is unchanged: only new files, never a file that is there.

### 5.8 Lanes (`ParagonLanes.swift`)

- As a note: after "Flow", per frame with lanes `## <frame name>` ("Lanes" when it has none), then per lane that holds something with words `### <lane name>` and its things as `- ` lines, in reading order (rows: left to right; columns: top to bottom). Empty lanes and frames with nothing in them are left out. Sticky notes and free text in a lane are listed there and also under "Notes" as before.
- As a project: per lane a task group `### <frame name>: <lane name>` (only `### <lane name>` when the frame has no name) with the lane's flow shapes as `- [ ]` tasks, placed before "### Steps"; those shapes are not repeated under "Steps". When the ticks decide (5.6) the lane groups follow them too: a ticked shape in a lane is `- [ ] `, an unticked one a plain `- ` line (`laneTasks(_:ticks:)`).

### 5.9 A timeline board (`Timeline.swift`, hooked into `Paragon.swift`)

- PARAGON's task line (read-only look at PARAGON build 339: `TaskParser.swift`, `Note.swift`) carries one day of its own: ` >2026-12-14` (PARAGON's "due"; the mark must stand alone between spaces or at the line's end). There is **no start or defer day** on a task. `@deadline(…)` exists, but PARAGON counts a task overdue from the earlier of the two, so "do on the first day, in by the last" would turn a running bar red on its second day. Therefore the bar's **last day** is written in PARAGON's own form and the first day in plain words.
- Task line: `- [ ] Foundation (starts 8 Dec) >2026-12-12`. The year is added to the words when start and end are in different years: `(starts 28 Dec 2026) >2027-01-06`. A one-day bar and a milestone: `- [ ] Roof on >2027-02-20`.
- As a project: front matter as in 5.5 plus `due: <last day on the timeline>` (PARAGON reads a project's own due date from that line); `## Outcome` = the title; `## Tasks` = the rows above the first phase, then `### <phase>` with its tasks, each group in date order (first day, then last day); `## Notes` = "- Walls comes after Foundation" lines; `## Log`.
- As a note: `## Timeline` (rows with dates, e.g. "Permit: 1 Dec – 5 Dec 2026 · 5 days"; a phase bold, its rows indented; "(milestone)" after a milestone's name), `## Comes after`, `## Tasks` (rows marked To do, as task lines with their dates). The picture is the timeline (`timelinePicture()`, 3.13).
- Only the to-do boxes: unchanged (`inboxTasks`): the rows marked To do go to the Inbox.
- Seedboard still only adds new files.

## 6. Storage and safety (BoardStore.swift; the decisions in SeedboardCore, Safety.swift)

- One file per board: `<UUID>.seedboard` (JSON, 3.2). Folders (device, inside the app's sandbox): Application Support/`Boards`, `Removed`, `SafetyCopies`. iCloud (container `iCloud.com.schabbauer.AMSSeedboard`, document scope public, name "Seedboard"): `Documents/Boards`, `Documents/Removed`. The cloud folder is asked for again every 30 s until found, so signing in later is noticed.
- Save: never makes the app wait, and the device never waits for iCloud. `BoardStore.save` hands the board to `BoardDisk.save`, which has two lines of work, each doing one thing at a time: `local` writes the device file at once (atomically), `cloud` does everything in iCloud's folder through file coordination. Several quick saves of one board become one iCloud write (the newest waiting version). `flush()` waits for the device line (2 s at most) when the app is left; iCloud's line gets background time (2.2). `cloudIsOn` is false without an iCloud account or in sample mode; the app then works from the device copy alone, and Settings says "iCloud is off" (2.5).
- What a board "grew from" (`Lineage`): for each board the store remembers the `changed` stamps of every version the app SHOWED (at loading, or news it took) and every version it saved, the newest 600. Two stamps less than 1.5 thousandths of a second apart are the same version (files keep thousandths).
- The rules:
  1. For the same board the newer `changed` wins; a losing version that differs is kept in `SafetyCopies` as `<UUID>-<yyyyMMdd-HHmmss>-<6 letters>.seedboard` (these single set-aside files are not removed by the 14-day rule but deleted after 30 days, 6.1; the same bytes are never kept twice). New in 0.2, in the NAME of a set-aside file only: the ending `-here` (a version this device showed or saved) or `-away` (a version another device wrote that was never shown here) before `.seedboard`; no ending = not known (the board had not been shown in this run of the app yet; all files of 0.1). Decided in `BoardDisk.setAside` from the lineage. Before a save writes over a stored version (on the device or in iCloud) that is NOT one this board grew from — another device wrote it and this app never showed it — that version is kept in `SafetyCopies` first (`SafetyRules.write`: write / keep the stored one then write / the stored one is newer and stays, mine is kept / nothing / refuse).
  2. A version that iCloud parked because two devices wrote at once (`NSFileVersion`) is settled BY ITSELF and only when it was really read and its words are safe: the newer wins (its own bytes go into the board's file), the other is kept in `SafetyCopies`; one that cannot be read as a board is kept there as `….unreadable`. A parked version that has not arrived, or cannot be dealt with now, stays for the next look. iCloud's own copies `<UUID> 2.seedboard` (any number) are read too and joined by the same rule (the loser to `SafetyCopies`), then the stray file is cleared away — only once its words are in the board's own file or in `SafetyCopies`. A copy without its board becomes the board. A copy that has not arrived (`.….icloud`) is asked for and joined at a later look. (`BoardFileName` reads what a file is from its name.)
  3. A file from a newer format (`format` above 3; for Seedboard 0.2: above 2, so every board 0.3 changed; for 0.1: above 1), or one holding a kind this version does not know, is read and SHOWN (`canBeChanged` false, read-only) and never written over (device and iCloud writers refuse it). When two copies differ and only one is such a file, that one wins and the other goes to the safety copies.
  4. A cloud file that cannot be read, or has not arrived yet, is never written over. A DEVICE file that cannot be read no longer stops every save: its bytes are kept in `SafetyCopies` (`….unreadable`), then the new version is written.
  5. A removed board leaves a mark `<UUID>.gone` in the device `Boards` folder and the iCloud `Boards` folder, and both copies move (device and iCloud) to `Removed/<UUID>-<yyyyMMdd-HHmmss>.seedboard` (the next free second when that name is taken). It is not deleted at once. The mark carries WHEN the board was removed: `{"removed":"<ISO 8601 with thousandths>"}`, always later than the board's last change (`RemovalMark.time`); an old mark of 0.1 is empty and counts as "removed at the mark file's date". At each load and each look at iCloud (`SafetyRules.removal`): a version of the board changed AFTER the removal wins — the marks are cleared on the device and in iCloud and the board is back on every device; otherwise every device that finds a mark moves its own copy to its Removed folder too (`moveAway`), and the board is not shown. A save of a marked board is refused unless its change is later than the removal (then it clears the mark). While a mark or iCloud's copy of the board has not arrived yet, nothing is decided for that board. Files in either Removed folder are deleted for good when the day in their NAME is more than 30 days back (`removedDay`); a file whose day cannot be read is kept. The 30 days count from when that device moved its copy. Marks are kept a year (by creation date). `BoardStore.bringBack(id:)` puts the newest copy in Removed back as the board, stamped later than the removal, and clears the marks; the buttons for it are "Bring back" on the page of 2.7 and on the removed-link line of 2.1 (`AppModel.bringBack(removed:)`, which then lets the board join the list and go through the usual save once more so iCloud has it).
  6. Once a day, before the first write or load of the day, all device boards are copied to `SafetyCopies/<yyyy-MM-dd>/`; the newest 14 day folders are kept. Nothing is made when there are no boards.
  7. Loading: the union of ids in both folders (a `.<name>.icloud` placeholder is asked for and counted), minus the removed ones (rule 5). Both copies equal → used; iCloud's newer → it is shown, the device's version goes to the safety copies and is replaced (unless the device has moved on meanwhile: then the next save settles it); the device's newer → it is shown and written to iCloud, and iCloud's version is kept in the safety copies unless it is one this board grew from (a newer-format copy beats an older-format one, rule 3); only the device copy → shown and sent to iCloud; only the cloud copy → shown and copied to the device; a side unreadable → the other is shown and the unreadable one is left alone. A board of a newer format is shown read-only (banner of 2.3).
  8. iCloud is watched (a query on `*.seedboard` and `*.gone`, and at every return to the app); a board that is newer than what the app SHOWS is news: it replaces the board in the list, and in the open board when it is not being typed in or dragged (then Undo's steps are cleared, 3.7). News for a board that is being typed in is held and offered again (2.2); only news the app shows counts as seen, anything else is offered again at the next look (`SafetyRules.news`: ignore / take / hold). Boards that gained a removal mark on another device leave the list (2.2).
  9. A removal reaches every device through the mark of rule 5: another device that still has its copy moves it to Removed at its next load or at the next look at iCloud — unless that copy was changed after the removal.
  10. Writes that fail are said, not swallowed. The store tells the app (`onState`) two things: `notInCloud` (rule 11) and `warning` (texts in 2.5) — a device write that failed, or an iCloud write that failed. While either is the case the store looks again every few seconds (3 s, then twice as long each time, 30 s at most): it tries the failed device write again, looks at iCloud again (which writes whatever is only on the device), and stops by itself when all is well.
  11. "Not in iCloud yet": a board is in this set from the moment it is saved until iCloud's folder holds that same version AND iCloud says the file is uploaded (`ubiquitousItemIsUploaded`); also boards found at loading that are only on the device or not uploaded. Without iCloud the set is empty (Settings says "iCloud is off" instead).
  12. Known weak spots (**Note**): a copy of a newer-format board is refused; Send on such a board writes the files in PARAGON but the sent mark is not kept. With clocks that disagree between devices the "newer" change is the one with the later stamp, not always the one made last; the loser is always in `SafetyCopies`.
- Mac: Settings → Show the boards folder opens the device `Boards` folder.

### 6.1 Removed boards and earlier versions (Kept.swift, KeptFiles.swift; the page: 2.7)

- `KeptRules.keepDays` = 30. `daysLeft(since:now:)` = 30 − days gone, rounded up, never below 0 (30 on the day of removal; 12 days gone → 18). 0 is shown as "last day", 1 as "1 day left".
- `letGo(kind, kept:, now:)` — removed boards and set-aside versions: older than 30 days; daily copies: never by this rule (they keep the newest 14 day folders, rule 6).
- `newest(_:)` — one copy per board: the version changed last wins; of two copies of the same version (stamps within 1.5 ms) the one removed last. Order: most recently removed first.
- `removedRows(_:present:)` — the newest copy of every board that is NOT in the list now (a board that was brought back leaves its old copies in the Removed folders until their 30 days are over; they are not shown).
- `groups(versions:boards:removed:marked:)` — per board: versions with the same `changed` stamp are one row (kept: set-aside with a known origin, before set-aside without, before daily; then the most recent copy); a version with the stamp of the board as it stands (or of the removed copy) is left out; a board with a removal mark and no removed copy left (removed for good, or its 30 days are over) is not listed; of `.setAside(.here)` only the newest `mostOwn` = 10 are listed, the rest counted in `hidden`. Order: boards in the list by name, then removed, then lost.
- `copyTitle` — "Title (version from 18 Nov 10:00)" (the VERSION's own date, i.e. its `changed`; the year added when not this year); a taken name gets " 2", " 3". `asCopy` — new identity, that title, created and changed now, not pinned, `sent` and `sentTodos` cleared. The board itself is never replaced.
- Words: `removedLine`, `sourceLine`, `clashLine` (2.1, 2.7). Dates are "d MMM" / "d MMM HH:mm" in English, with the year when it is not this year.
- `KeptFileName` reads a kept file's name: `Removed/<id>-<yyyyMMdd-HHmmss>.seedboard`; `SafetyCopies/<id>-<yyyyMMdd-HHmmss>-<6 letters>[-here|-away].seedboard` (or `.unreadable`); `.<name>.icloud` = not arrived.
- `KeptFiles.list` — reads both Removed folders (device, iCloud), SafetyCopies (day folders `yyyy-MM-dd/<id>.seedboard` and set-aside files) and the removal marks in both Boards folders. A copy is listed only when it decodes as the board its name says.
- `tidy(safety:now:)` — deletes set-aside files (`.seedboard` and `.unreadable`) older than 30 days; called at every look at the folders (`BoardDisk.loadAll`, after `purgeRemoved`). (In 0.1 set-aside versions were never cleared away.)
- `removeForGood(id, in:)` — deletes every file of that board in the Removed folders, nothing else. The removal mark stays (so no device puts the board back); the board's safety copies stay until their own time is up but are no longer listed.
- `read(version, in:)` — reads one earlier version again (only ever a file inside SafetyCopies).
- The store's bridge: `BoardStore.kept { places in … }` runs such work on the store's iCloud line of work, off the main thread; nil with the example boards (nothing is read or written then). `BoardStore.bytes(of:)` / `board(from:)` give the board's file coding.
- **Note**: "where it came from" is the device's best knowledge within one run of the app. A version set aside before the board was shown in that run says only "set aside". In a rare order of events (iCloud's copy not readable at the first look after a restart, and the device ahead of it) the device's own older version in iCloud can be taken for another device's and raise the clash line once without need.
- **Note**: a clash where the device was restarted before it noticed (its unsent change loses against a newer one from iCloud at the next start) is kept under Earlier versions, but raises no line.
- **Note**: this device's version is set aside every time another device's change arrives, so a board edited on two devices collects many "this device's version" rows; the page lists the newest 10 per board and counts the rest. All are deleted after 30 days. The old hook `AppModel.bringBack(_:)` is no longer called and may be removed.

### 6.2 Export and import (ExportAll.swift; `AppModel.exportAllBoards`, `importBoards`)

- Export all boards (Settings, 2.5). Name: "Seedboard boards yyyy-MM-dd" + ".zip". Inside one folder of the same name: per board `<name>.seedboard` (the same bytes as the board's own file) and `<name>.md`. `<name>` = the title cleaned by PARAGON's file-name rule (`ParagonExport.noteTitle`); a second board with the same name (whatever the capitals) gets " (2)", " (3)". **0.3:** the folder also holds every picture file a board names (`<UUID>.jpg`, the stored bytes, read with `PictureStore.fileBytes`; a file that is not on this device is left out). Import boards: a chosen `.jpg` with a plain name is taken back under its own name (`PictureStore.adopt`; one that is already there stays), and the line says "1 picture came in." / "N pictures came in.".
- The `.md`: "# Title", a line "counts · last changed 17 Nov 10:00 · exported from Seedboard on 18 Nov", the board's outline (`Board.outlineText`, 3.10) and "To do:" with `- [ ]` lines for everything marked to do.
- The packed file is made by the system (`NSFileCoordinator`, reading the folder `.forUploading`) in the app's temporary folder, which is cleared again; nothing third-party. It is handed over with SwiftUI's `fileExporter` (Files' save sheet on iPhone and iPad, a save panel on the Mac).
- "Last export" is stored per device in the app's settings, key `lastExport` (seconds since 1970), only after the save place said yes. What is being typed is kept first, so it is in the export.
- Import boards… (`ExportRules.imported`, `looksLikeBoard`): any files may be chosen (several at once); a file counts as a board when it is JSON with `id`, `title` and `items` and this version can change it. Each becomes a new board: new identity, changed now, not pinned, not sent; a taken name gets " (imported)", " (imported 2)". The line below says "2 boards came in as new boards." and/or "1 file was not a board this Seedboard can read." The packed file itself cannot be chosen: the owner unpacks it first.

## 7. From outside, and opening where you left

### 7.1 Shortcuts actions (`Intents.swift`, App Intents, iOS 17 / macOS 14)

- `NewBoardFromTextIntent` "New board from text": Title (optional), Text (several lines). The board is made by `Outside.board(title:text:)` in SeedboardCore — the same rule as pasting: two or more non-empty lines and a readable outline → a mind map (`addMap`; one top line = the middle idea, several = the title, or "Ideas", in the middle); else one sticky note; no text → an empty board. Name: the title, else the middle idea, else the first line (40 letters at most). Gives the new board back, so a shortcut can go on with it.
- `NewBoardIntent` "New board": **0.5:** "Start with" offers the owner's whole shelf (`StarterEntity`), empty = blank; see 11.17, which also adds Add to board, Board as text, Board as picture and Find Boards. (0.4: the seven built-in starters only.)
- `OpenBoardIntent` "Open board": Board = one of the boards, offered newest first, found by name (`Outside.boards(named:)`: the exact name, then names that begin with the words, then names that hold them). Opens it the way a link is followed, so a board removed meanwhile is said so in the app.
- All three open the app and run inside it, through the app's one model (`AppModel.shared`; `AppModel.add` gives the colour, a name of its own "… 2", saves through the store and opens). There is no second writer. They wait until the boards are read, so they work when the app was not running. Problem text: "The board could not be made."
- `SeedboardShortcuts` (`AppShortcutsProvider`), 7 phrases, each with the app's name: "New Seedboard board from text", "Start a thought in Seedboard", "New Seedboard board", "New board in Seedboard", "New <starter> in Seedboard", "Open a Seedboard board", "Open <board> in Seedboard". The board names Siri offers are refreshed when the list of names changes. Raycast runs the actions through its Shortcuts command.
- 0.3: the "New board" action and its phrases still offer only the seven built-in starters (`StarterChoice`, as in 0.2); the owner's own starters and the changes he made to built-in ones are not used there.
- No Share-sheet action of its own; "New board from text" can be used from the Share sheet through a shortcut that takes the shared text.

### 7.2 Search on the device (`Spotlight.swift`, Core Spotlight)

- **0.5:** the entry is told every word (up to 20 000 letters) and carries the board's small picture; key `seedboard.searchTold.2` (11.17). The 0.4 text follows.
- One entry per board: id = the board's id, name = the board's name, description = the first words (`Outside.searchWords`: every thing's words in the order made, one line each, joined by " · ", cut at a whole word at 300 letters, then "…"; an empty board shows its count line), never expiring.
- Told 2 s after the list of boards last changed, only for boards whose time of last change differs from what was told before (`Outside.searchPlan`; kept in `UserDefaults`, key `seedboard.searchTold`), on a background task. Boards no longer in the list are taken out of the search. With `-sampleData` nothing is told.
- Choosing a result opens the board through the same path as a `seedboard://board/<id>` link (`onContinueUserActivity`).

### 7.3 Opens where you left (`Core/Remember.swift`, `App/Sources/Remember.swift`)

- Kept in the device's own settings (`UserDefaults`, key `seedboard.remembered`, JSON): `open` (id of the open board, absent on the home page) and `views` (board id → `scale`, `x`, `y`). `x`, `y` is the board point that stood in the MIDDLE of the room (rounded to 0.1), `scale` the zoom (rounded to 0.001) — the middle, so the same spot comes back in a window of another size. Never synced, never in a board file.
- Written: when another board is opened or the board is closed, 0.6 s after the view last moved, and when the app is left or quit. A view is only written once the owner has moved or zoomed the board; before that the board keeps opening shown whole. Views of boards that are no longer on the device are dropped at each write.
- At the start (`AppModel.load`): `Reopen.board` gives the board to open — the remembered one if it is on the device, else none (home page). A link, a Shortcuts action or a search result that started the app wins over it.
- Whenever a board is opened: `Reopen.view` gives its view; `Viewport.comeBack` sets the zoom (kept within 20 %–300 %) and holds the remembered spot in the middle while the room still settles (window finding its size, side bar), until the owner slides or zooms (`Viewport.pin`, `holdPin`).
- Launch words win: with any of `-sampleData`, `-board`, `-pick`, `-pickAll`, `-send`, `-sendNow`, `-settings`, `-starters` nothing is brought back (`Reopen.sceneWords`); with `-sampleData` nothing is written down either.

## 8. Launch arguments (pictures and tests)

| Argument | Effect |
| --- | --- |
| `-sampleData` | Made-up boards (Summer cabin pinned, App ideas pinned, Garage shelves, Trip to Spain, Packing flow, Training year, Workshop bench "sent"; new in 0.2: Build timeline, Arrow looks, Lanes and shapes), nothing read or written, PARAGON "not found". Sample "Summer cabin": 12 ideas, 5 shapes, 2 notes, two to-do items. The UI tests expect "12 ideas · 5 shapes · 2 notes". Plain `-sampleData` shows the home page on Mac and iPad and the list with pictures on the iPhone. |
| `-board <word>` | Opens the first board whose title contains the word (case-insensitive). `-board timeline` (Build timeline), `-board Arrow` (Arrow looks), `-board Lanes` (Lanes and shapes), `-board Packing` (coloured Flow), `-board Garage` (coloured Plan in steps). |
| `-pick <word>` | In the opened board, chooses the first item whose words contain the word (on a timeline: the row). |
| `-pickAll <words>` | Chooses every thing whose words hold one of the comma-separated words, e.g. `-board cabin -pickAll "New idea,Worth,Make a plan"` → the bar with Line up, Even gaps, Same size. |
| `-pickArrow <word>` | Chooses the first arrow whose words hold it, e.g. `-board Arrow -pickArrow skip` → the end dots and the arrow's bar. |
| `-guides` | Shows the pink guides of the chosen thing(s) where they stand (no drag needed), e.g. `-board cabin -pick Worth -guides`. |
| `-colours` | With `-pick`: the colour dots are open (two rows on the iPhone). |
| `-type` | Starts typing in the picked thing, e.g. `-board cabin -pick Loan -type` → the keyboard bar (iPhone/iPad; the simulator must show its screen keyboard). |
| `-plate shapes` / `-plate shape` / `-plate lanes` | The plate with all shapes over the tool pill; with `-pick` of a shape the "Change the shape" plate; with `-pick` of a frame the Lanes plate. |
| `-lit` | With `-pick`: the lane (or frame) under the picked thing is lit as if it were being dragged, e.g. `-board Lanes -pick "Order comes" -lit`. |
| `-scale days\|weeks\|months` | Timeline: that scale, with today a third of the way in. |
| `-dates` | Timeline, with `-pick`: opens the Dates page of that bar. |
| `-search <word>` | Fills the search field, e.g. `-search timber` → only "Summer cabin"; `-search zzz` → "No board has these words". |
| `-starters` | Shows the starter page of File ▸ New Board. |
| `-starterPage` | With `-sampleData`: opens the Starters page (2.10) over the main window; the sample shelf has two own starters, "Weekly review" and "Trip plan". |
| `-starterChange <id or name>` | With `-sampleData`: opens that starter for changing, with the banner "Changing the starter “…”", e.g. `-starterChange mindMap` or `-starterChange "Trip plan"` (a built-in's raw value, an own starter's id, or its name, case-insensitive). |
| `-board balcony` | With `-sampleData`: the made-up board "Balcony makeover" (two pictures, two notes, one arrow; 3.17). |
| `-pickPicture` | With the board above: chooses the first picture, so its bar shows (handled in `PictureHost`). |
| `-cropPicture` | With the board above: chooses the first picture and opens its "Crop the picture" page (0.4; `PictureHost`). |
| `-send` | Opens the Send page. |
| `-sendAs note\|project\|todos` | With `-send`: chooses that row, e.g. `-board cabin -send -sendAs project` → the project line. |
| `-branchOnly` | With `-pick` of an idea and `-send`: the branch switch is on. |
| `-settings` | Opens Settings (with the new rows in Your boards). |
| `-removed` / `-removed <word>` | Opens "Removed and earlier versions" over the main window (on the Mac a sheet), with made-up content from `SampleKept.swift`: two removed boards ("Old garden plan", 18 days left; "Birthday party", 3 days left) and "Summer cabin" with three earlier versions; with a word, opened and scrolled at that board. |
| `-clash` | Shows the clash line for the first board. |
| `-draw` | The Draw tool on and its plate showing, e.g. `-sampleData -board Sketch -draw` (the made-up board "Sketch ideas", `SampleSketch.swift`: a floor plan, "Lake side" in a red ring, a tick; 2 notes, 3 sketches). |
| `-board Loan` | The made-up "Loan steps" board (`SequenceSample.swift`); with `-pick "Order the build"` the message bar, `-pick Bank` the name bar, `-pick Sundays` the note, `-pick refused` the section; `-type` with `-pick` starts typing in it; `-send -sendAs project` shows the Send page with the sequence words. |
| `-pickArrow`, `-pickAll`, `-more`, `-plate line\|ends` | The bar's pictures: `-board Summer -pick Worth` (shape), `-pick Loan` (idea), `-pick Summer` (middle idea), `-pick timber` (note), `-pickArrow Yes` (arrow), `-pickAll "New idea,Worth,plan"` (several), `-board Lanes -pick "Web shop"` (frame), `-board Arrow -pick Stock` (no plus on a side with an arrow). `-more` opens the "…" list (iPhone, only when the bar has one); `-plate line` or `-plate ends` with `-pickArrow` shows the arrow's two lists. |
| `-menu` | 1.2 s after the board shows, opens the menu of the picked thing (or picked arrow; with nothing picked: of the spot 30 % across, 25 % down). iPhone / iPad: the plate. Mac: the system's real menu, which stays until closed. |
| `-night` | Night colours, whatever the system says. |
| `-paragonFolder <path>` | Debug builds only. Treats that folder as PARAGON's notes (it must hold `Inbox.md` or `Projects/`) and uses `<path>/.seedboard-test-outbox/capture-outbox.jsonl` as the waiting list. Works together with `-sampleData`. |
| `-sendNow note\|project\|todos` | Debug builds only. Sends the opened board at once (with `-paragonFolder`) and prints the result. |
| `-ApplePersistenceIgnoreState YES` | Mac tests: no restored windows. |

`-sampleData` goes last in the UI tests' argument list. Screen tests find controls by the identifiers in section 2. The menus themselves, the landing line of an idea drag and the colour ring's row need a click or a drag to be photographed.

## 9. Not built, and not proven on a real device

### 9.1 Parts that were written last

- Pencil drawing (pick 2; 3.15), the sequence diagram "Who does what" (pick 3; 3.16) and the bar beside the chosen thing, the menu of a thing, copy / cut / paste and the Home row (picks 7, 17, 18; 2.9) are built and written down. Nothing of them was run on a device (9.4).

### 9.2 Picks of the panel with no builder's note at the time of writing

Picks 17 (copy, cut, paste between boards; dropped text; Option-drag; a finger's choosing box) and 18 (the right-click / press-and-hold menu) ARE built now (2.9). The ones below are still not built:

- Pick 9 (tool pill with a word under each tile, drag a tool out, hold Box for Round/Oval — the last part is covered by the shape plate); pick 22 (find in a board, "Only this branch", fit down to 20 %, fold by pressing the dot); pick 23 (a note on an idea, "See also" links); pick 27 (fast on big boards; only the arrows' own cache is there).

### 9.3 What 0.2 does not do

- (0.2) No pictures, files or links on a board. 0.3 has pictures (3.17); there are still no files or links on a board.
- No shared editing: boards are the owner's own across his devices; there are no other people, no comments.
- No folders or tags; no templates beyond the starters (the seven built-in ones and the owner's own, 3.18).
- No press-and-hold menu on a board card (the Home row of 2.9 is the way back to the home page); no "Bring back a removed board…" outside Settings.
- Import does not read the packed export file itself: the owner unpacks it first (the system offers a way to make such a file, none to open one).
- A branch that was sent leaves no trace on the board (no "Last sent branch").
- Move up / Move down are not in the Mac's bar (keys and the Board menu do it). "Fold" is not in the Board menu. No letter keys for the four new shapes.
- A timeline does not remember its scale and place; a list cannot be pasted onto it; a phase's rows do not travel with it; "New board" in Shortcuts does not offer Timeline.
- No inertia after a two-finger slide. Other screens than the board (list, Settings, Send page) keep their button sizes, apart from `FilledButton`. An arrow's catch area is 9 screen points for a finger too.
- ⌘ switches falling in line off on the Mac only. A shape that gets taller as its words are typed grows downward, so its middle moves and a straight arrow can get a kink.
- Long words on a short arrow can run on under the next shape; with three arrows on one side the middle one's word can touch the upper line.
- The text for PARAGON and "Copy the board as text" write `→` for every arrow, also Both ways and No ends; "Copy the board as text" does not list lanes.
- iPhone and iPad with a finger: no choosing box and no Shift (use Choose more in the bar); no ⌘V (use ⇧⌘V with a keyboard, or More → Paste a list as a mind map).
- Known weak spots of storage: 6, rule 12, and the last three points of 6.1.

### 9.4 Only a real device, or a real run, can prove

(0.3's own list is 9.5. This part is the list of 0.2, which still stands.)

Nothing of 0.2 was launched by its builders: both apps compile, the rules are tested as data, the look was checked in off-screen drawings only. The screen tests are written and compiled, not run.

- Touch (iPhone, iPad): a press still begins the moment the finger lands, and taps, drags, the 0.25 s hold and typing feel as in 0.1; two fingers slide and zoom together without a jump at the start, when a finger is lifted and when it comes back; the first finger's shove is really taken back and the selection does not flicker; the press areas really reach beyond the look (44 points); the swipe back from the left edge still goes back and leaves the board untouched (on iOS 26 a sideways slide on the board must not pop the page); typing zoom to 100 % and back with the keyboard coming and going; a big board opening at 75 % in both orientations.
- iPad trackpad and mouse: two-finger scroll direction and speed, pinch round the pointer, the mouse wheel's step, ⌘ + wheel; a click arriving as a pointer touch (choosing box, idea follows at once), Shift-click.
- Pencil: taps and drags reach choose / move; a resting hand does not disturb; the Pencil taking over from a finger.
- Drawing (needs a real iPad with a Pencil): the Pencil on empty board draws at once with no felt delay, also on a long line and a busy board; no jump when the tip lifts; a resting hand leaves nothing (also when it lands before the Pencil); the pressure feel (the curve is a first guess); double-tap and squeeze follow Settings, Apple Pencil, and the "The Pencil rubs out" line appears; Only Draw with Apple Pencil on; a Pencil tap on a sketch's line chooses it, a drag across draws, a chosen sketch moves; Scribble into an open text field; one finger draws with Draw on and a second finger throws the line away; a click-drag with an iPad trackpad or mouse; on the Mac a trackpad drag draws smoothly and Escape leaves the tool.
- Who does what: pulling a message with a finger (is 11 points enough to catch the dashed line); the 0.25 s rest before a message lifts; a pinch while a drag is on; the keyboard bar buttons on an iPhone; Tab reaching the field on an iPad with a keyboard; the pinned names while scrolling fast on 120 Hz; the first real Send of a sequence into the real notes.
- The bar and the menu: on the Mac Edit > Cut / Copy / Paste turn black and act while the board has the keys and stay with the words while typing, and Delete and Select All still work; on an iPad with a keyboard ⌘C / ⌘X / ⌘V act on the board and still copy and paste words in a text field (board words, search, Send page); right-click and Control-click open the menu on a thing, an arrow and empty board but not on the tools, the resize arrow and the hand show, nothing slows a click or a drag; on the iPhone and iPad the half-second press opens the plate, holding an idea a little shorter and dragging still lifts it, moving after the plate opened turns into a drag, a two-finger click on a trackpad; the bar sits right on Mac and iPad at every zoom, hides and returns, is one row on every iPhone width, and its "…" sheet and pop-ups work; paste from Seedboard into Notes / Mail (words) and Preview / Pages (picture), and text copied elsewhere (iOS may ask "Allow Paste"); text dragged in from Safari on Mac, iPad (side by side) and iPhone; sizing from each of the four corners with snapping and with long words.
- Falling in line: how the 6-point catch feels under a finger (too sticky / too weak); that the guides are easy to see by day and by night; that the dots look soft, not busy, especially by night, and that dragging stays smooth on a large Mac window at 50 % zoom; ⌘ held during a drag on the Mac; the dashed landing outline.
- Mind maps: the keyboard really stays up from idea to idea, with autocorrect and with dictation (`fieldIdentity` in `MapOrder.swift` is the one place; returning `session.editing` there gives 0.1's behaviour back); the keyboard bar's four buttons fit on the smallest iPhone; dragging between sisters with a finger, the "hang" middle of a one-line sister against the gaps, and the 120-point tear-off at 50 % and 200 % zoom; on the Mac ⌘↑ / ⌘↓ still move the cursor while typing in a box, a note, the rename box and the Send page's name field, and move the idea while typing in an idea; on the iPad with a keyboard they move a chosen idea.
- Arrows: Tab while typing in a shape (if it only ends the typing, `session.flowKey(press)` must be asked first in the field's own Tab handler); ⌘ + arrow while typing a shape makes the next shape instead of moving the cursor; ⌘↑ / ⌘↓ on a chosen shape on the Mac (2.6, **Note**); dragging the end dots with a finger (catch area 13 points) and with the mouse; the look of dashes and dots at 50 % and 300 %.
- Shapes and lanes: hold against press on the shape tile on iPhone, iPad and with the Mac's pointer; that typing begins in the new shape after a pick from the pop-up and from the sheet; the sheet's height on the iPhone (230 for shapes, 250 for lanes) with the largest text size; a lane's name being kept when you press elsewhere, and Tab going on to the next lane; dragging a lane's line with a finger, and the Mac's resize pointer over it; the look of the upright lane names and of the four shapes by night.
